Yes… yes, I think so.

I was giving a talk there, and I got up on one of the tractors to, um, demonstrate something I thought was important — a “new pattern,” I called it. I was moving the controls around, probably more than I should’ve, and people were yelling for me to stop.\n\nI don’t mean I was trying to scare anyone. I just… couldn’t seem to make myself quit right then. So they thought I ought to come in and talk to somebody. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you used amphetamines or methamphetamine recently?\n\nPatient: No, sir, I don’t believe so. I mean, not anything like that. I try to stay away from that kind of thing. Is that… is that what people were thinking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t want to make too much of it, but yes, sometimes. Mostly out by the north pasture, near dusk. I’ll hear voices pretty clear, like they’re coming through the irrigation lines, telling me which rows are poisoned or blessed.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just… it happens even on weeks when I’m not all worked up or full of energy. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose there have been mornings like that. Not all the time, but some mornings I wake up and it feels like the whole valley’s opening up just for me, like everything’s clear and I’m grinning at nothing.\n\nI don’t mean to say I’m being foolish or anything. It feels good at first. But then if one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me to slow down, I can… well, I can snap pretty quick, like they’ve insulted the family name. I’m not proud of that. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear those voices even when you are not feeling especially high or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but there were some quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially big or full of energy or anything like that.\n\nAnd even then, I’d still hear the lines talking sometimes… and I’d still get the sense that the farm data had hidden warnings in it. Not all wild and worked up, just… still there. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and hidden-warning beliefs been present for longer than one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… more than a month, yes. I probably should’ve said that clearer.\n\nIt’s been going on, off and on, for about eleven months now — the voices and those… hidden warnings I think I’m seeing. I don’t mean every minute of every day, but it’s been around that long. Is that the sort of timeframe you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least a week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there have been stretches like that, at least close to it. Some mornings I’d wake up feeling like the whole valley was opening up for me, and I’d be grinning at nothing, like everything was going my way.\n\nI don’t like saying I was irritable, but… if a hired hand questioned me, or my wife told me to slow down, I could snap pretty quick, like they’d insulted the family name. It wasn’t my best self. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable stretches, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I did. I mean, I’d tell myself I was just resting my eyes, but most nights I’d only get maybe two or three hours in the chair… sometimes I don’t think I slept at all.\n\nAnd then I’d still be up before the sun, feeling charged up, ready to work the place like nothing had happened. It didn’t feel bad at the time, if that makes sense… more like I was just finally keeping up. Is that what you mean by needing less sleep?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voices or hidden warnings were happening for at least two weeks while your mood was otherwise normal—not especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling all fired up or grand about things, and I wasn’t especially low either. Just… more like myself, I guess. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking, and I still felt like the farm data was carrying warnings in it.\n\nI didn’t make as much of a fuss about it then, because I figured I was handling it better, but it was still there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed for two whole weeks, Doctor. I’ve had rough spells, sure… everybody does, especially when things pile up. But most of the day, nearly every day, for that long? I’m not sure.\n\nMaybe there were times I was quieter or more worn down than usual, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was. Is there a certain kind of “depressed” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt down or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure I can say that, no.\n\nI’ve had days where I felt kind of hollow or worn out, sure, but for two weeks straight, most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I’ve noticed it like that. I don’t want to overstate it just to give the “right” answer.\n\nMaybe I’m not thinking about it the way you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time these problems have been going on, have the times when your mood was high or irritable made up most of it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose if I’m being honest, a lot of it has been that way.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound out of control or anything, but for most of this stretch I’ve been kind of… charged up, driven, like I had to keep pushing and making big plans. I was giving a talk there, and I got up on one of the tractors to, um, demonstrate something I thought was important — a “new pattern,” I called it. I was moving the controls around, probably more than I should’ve, and people were yelling for me to stop.\n\nI don’t mean I was trying to scare anyone. I just… couldn’t seem to make myself quit right then. So they thought I ought to come in and talk to somebody. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you used amphetamines or methamphetamine recently?\n\nPatient: No, sir, I don’t believe so. I mean, not anything like that. I try to stay away from that kind of thing. Is that… is that what people were thinking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t want to make too much of it, but yes, sometimes. Mostly out by the north pasture, near dusk. I’ll hear voices pretty clear, like they’re coming through the irrigation lines, telling me which rows are poisoned or blessed.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just… it happens even on weeks when I’m not all worked up or full of energy. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose there have been mornings like that. Not all the time, but some mornings I wake up and it feels like the whole valley’s opening up just for me, like everything’s clear and I’m grinning at nothing.\n\nI don’t mean to say I’m being foolish or anything. It feels good at first. But then if one of the hired hands questions what I’m doing, or my wife tells me to slow down, I can… well, I can snap pretty quick, like they’ve insulted the family name. I’m not proud of that. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear those voices even when you are not feeling especially high or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but there were some quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially big or full of energy or anything like that.\n\nAnd even then, I’d still hear the lines talking sometimes… and I’d still get the sense that the farm data had hidden warnings in it. Not all wild and worked up, just… still there. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and hidden-warning beliefs been present for longer than one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… more than a month, yes. I probably should’ve said that clearer.\n\nIt’s been going on, off and on, for about eleven months now — the voices and those… hidden warnings I think I’m seeing. I don’t mean every minute of every day, but it’s been around that long. Is that the sort of timeframe you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least a week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there have been stretches like that, at least close to it. Some mornings I’d wake up feeling like the whole valley was opening up for me, and I’d be grinning at nothing, like everything was going my way.\n\nI don’t like saying I was irritable, but… if a hired hand questioned me, or my wife told me to slow down, I could snap pretty quick, like they’d insulted the family name. It wasn’t my best self. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable stretches, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I did. I mean, I’d tell myself I was just resting my eyes, but most nights I’d only get maybe two or three hours in the chair… sometimes I don’t think I slept at all.\n\nAnd then I’d still be up before the sun, feeling charged up, ready to work the place like nothing had happened. It didn’t feel bad at the time, if that makes sense… more like I was just finally keeping up. Is that what you mean by needing less sleep?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voices or hidden warnings were happening for at least two weeks while your mood was otherwise normal—not especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling all fired up or grand about things, and I wasn’t especially low either. Just… more like myself, I guess. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking, and I still felt like the farm data was carrying warnings in it.\n\nI didn’t make as much of a fuss about it then, because I figured I was handling it better, but it was still there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed for two whole weeks, Doctor. I’ve had rough spells, sure… everybody does, especially when things pile up. But most of the day, nearly every day, for that long? I’m not sure.\n\nMaybe there were times I was quieter or more worn down than usual, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was. Is there a certain kind of “depressed” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt down or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure I can say that, no.\n\nI’ve had days where I felt kind of hollow or worn out, sure, but for two weeks straight, most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I’ve noticed it like that. I don’t want to overstate it just to give the “right” answer.\n\nMaybe I’m not thinking about it the way you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time these problems have been going on, have the times when your mood was high or irritable made up most of it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose if I’m being honest, a lot of it has been that way.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound out of control or anything, but for most of this stretch I’ve been kind of… charged up, driven, like I had to keep pushing and making big plans. And I know I got irritable too, especially when people tried to slow me down or question me.\n\nSo… yes, I think those high or edgy times probably have made up most of it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable periods, did your behavior ever get severe enough that you had to be hospitalized?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I don’t think I’d say it got to the point where I had to be hospitalized, at least not that I’m remembering that way.\n\nThere were times people were worried about me, sure, but being put in the hospital for it… I don’t know. I don’t want to say no if I’m misunderstanding what counts, but I can’t really point to that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I guess it did. I don’t like saying it that way, because I’ve always handled the farm and I don’t want to sound incapable.

But my wife and the workers have had to step in more—making decisions I used to make about lenders, equipment, day-to-day operations. They’ve said it just doesn’t feel safe leaving me fully in charge when I’m like that.

And after what happened at the fair, people really started questioning whether I could keep running the farm at all. So… yes, I suppose it caused serious problems.

or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I'll be balancing feed costs or talking to my wife, then a crow call, a blinking sensor light, or a truck on the road pulls me off and I'm chasing I’ll hear voices pretty clear, like they’re coming through the irrigation lines, telling me which rows are poisoned or blessed.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. It’s just… it happens even on weeks when I’m not all worked up or full of energy. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose there have been mornings like that. Not all the time, but some mornings I wake up and it feels like the whole valley’s opening up just for me, like everything’s clear and I’m grinning at nothing.\n\nI don’t mean to say I’m being foolish or anything. I don’t want to overstate it, but there were some quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially big or full of energy or anything like that.\n\nAnd even then, I’d still hear the lines talking sometimes… and I’d still get the sense that the farm data had hidden warnings in it. Not all wild and worked up, just… still there. I don’t mean every minute of every day, but it’s been around that long. Is that the sort of timeframe you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least a week when you felt unusually high or irritable most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there have been stretches like that, at least close to it. Some mornings I’d wake up feeling like the whole valley was opening up for me, and I’d be grinning at nothing, like everything was going my way.\n\nI don’t like saying I was irritable, but… if a hired hand questioned me, or my wife told me to slow down, I could snap pretty quick, like they’d insulted the family name. It wasn’t my best self. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable stretches, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I did. I mean, I’d tell myself I was just resting my eyes, but most nights I’d only get maybe two or three hours in the chair… sometimes I don’t think I slept at all.\n\nAnd then I’d still be up before the sun, feeling charged up, ready to work the place like nothing had happened. It didn’t feel bad at the time, if that makes sense… more like I was just finally keeping up. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable stretches, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I did. I mean, I’d tell myself I was just resting my eyes, but most nights I’d only get maybe two or three hours in the chair… sometimes I don’t think I slept at all.\n\nAnd then I’d still be up before the sun, feeling charged up, ready to work the place like nothing had happened. It didn’t feel bad at the time, if that makes sense… more like I was just finally keeping up. Is that what you mean by needing less sleep?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the voices or hidden warnings were happening for at least two weeks while your mood was otherwise normal—not especially high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling all fired up or grand about things, and I wasn’t especially low either. Just… more like myself, I guess. But even then, I could still hear the lines talking, and I still felt like the farm data was carrying warnings in it.\n\nI didn’t make as much of a fuss about it then, because I figured I was handling it better, but it was still there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed for two whole weeks, Doctor. I’ve had rough spells, sure… everybody does, especially when things pile up. But most of the day, nearly every day, for that long? I’m not sure.\n\nMaybe there were times I was quieter or more worn down than usual, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was. Is there a certain kind of “depressed” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt down or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure I can say that, no.\n\nI’ve had days where I felt kind of hollow or worn out, sure, but for two weeks straight, most of the day nearly every day… I don’t know that I’ve noticed it like that. I don’t want to overstate it just to give the “right” answer.\n\nMaybe I’m not thinking about it the way you mean, though.\n\nDoctor: Over the whole time these problems have been going on, have the times when your mood was high or irritable made up most of it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose if I’m being honest, a lot of it has been that way.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound out of control or anything, but for most of this stretch I’ve been kind of… charged up, driven, like I had to keep pushing and making big plans. And I know I got irritable too, especially when people tried to slow me down or question me.\n\nSo… yes, I think those high or edgy times probably have made up most of it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During those high or irritable periods, did your behavior ever get severe enough that you had to be hospitalized?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I don’t think I’d say it got to the point where I had to be hospitalized, at least not that I’m remembering that way.\n\nThere were times people were worried about me, sure, but being put in the hospital for it… I don’t know. I don’t want to say no if I’m misunderstanding what counts, but I can’t really point to that.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high or irritable periods, were you ever admitted to a hospital because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not admitted, no.\n\nI mean, people may have been concerned, and I know I wasn’t always easy to deal with, but actually going into the hospital because of being high or irritable—I can’t say that happened. If there’s a specific kind of admission you mean, I might need you to explain it, but as far as I know, no.\n\nDoctor: During one of those high or irritable periods, did it cause serious problems in your life or work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess it did. I don’t like saying it that way, because I’ve always handled the farm and I don’t want to sound incapable.\n\nBut my wife and the workers have had to step in more—making decisions I used to make about lenders, equipment, day-to-day operations. They’ve said it just doesn’t feel safe leaving me fully in charge when I’m like that.\n\nAnd after what happened at the fair, people really started questioning whether I could keep running the farm at all. So… yes, I suppose it caused serious problems.\n\nDoctor: When the voices are happening during those quieter times, are you otherwise feeling emotionally about your usual self?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, I wasn’t all worked up or feeling especially big or energized during some of those times.\n\nThere were a few quieter weeks where I felt more like myself emotionally, at least as far as I could tell. But I still heard the lines talking, and I still thought the farm data had hidden warnings in it. I know that may sound odd… I’m just trying to answer it plainly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. So I don’t want to give you the wrong answer—maybe not that I can clearly say.\n\nDoctor: Before these past eleven months, had you ever heard the voices or noticed those hidden warnings at a time when you were not feeling high or irritable?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can remember before this past year.
